- Active motifs: appointed time for winds to blow (`quranic:root_001210:B005/m01`); wind called dead when it becomes still (`quranic:root_001454:B012/m01`); vessel brought to a standstill at sea (`quranic:root_000114:B003/m01`); captain or master of sailors (`quranic:root_000532:B017/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 75:17-18 `قُرْءَانَهُۥ`, `قَرَأْنَٰهُ` (ق ر ء); 75:40 `ٱلْمَوْتَىٰ` (م و ت); 75:24 `بَاسِرَةٌ` (ب س ر); 75:12,23,30 `رَبِّكَ`, `رَبِّهَا`, `رَبِّكَ` (ر ب ب).
- Synthesis: The captain's expertise cannot restore the force that carries the vessel once the wind dies. This reframes the surah's no-refuge and terminal-driving scenes: when motion is arrested, skilled command cannot create escape, and the final destination remains with the Lord.

### S27. P1-P2-P3 Bridge: Form Assembled, Severed, and Re-formed
- Reading type: mixed
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (75:3-4) -> P2 (75:28-29) -> P3 (75:37-40); causal sequence and reversal: ordered form is assembled from parts, its living relations are severed at death, and origination supplies the precedent for exact re-formation.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 2A-2B; P2 18A and 18C; P3 33A-33C and 37B.
- Scene or process: The surah states bodily recovery, passes through the inverse operations of separation and terminal wrapping, then returns to the body's first formation and the capacity to produce the recovered outcome.
- Active motifs: dispersed parts collected into one (`quranic:root_000259:B001/m01`); bodily completeness without loss (`quranic:root_000259:B009/m01`); connected parts distinguished and severed (`quranic:root_001148:B001/m01`); dead body wrapped and folded (`quranic:root_001365:B001/m01`); origination of created form (`quranic:root_000434:B002/m01`); completed and proportioned image (`quranic:root_000434:B003/m01`); effective capability that reaches the act (`quranic:root_001205:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 75:3 `نَّجْمَعَ` (ج م ع); 75:28 `ٱلْفِرَاقُ` (ف ر ق); 75:29 `ٱلْتَفَّتِ` (ل ف ف); 75:38 `خَلَقَ` (خ ل ق); 75:40 `قَٰدِرٍ` (ق د ر).
- Synthesis: P1 announces the recovered whole before P2 displays the body's unmaking; P3 then runs back to generative origin and forward to renewed capacity. The bridge is therefore a reversal of bodily process, not a repeated body theme: severance exposes the relations that assembly and creation must restore.

### S28. P1-P2-P3 Bridge: Chosen Motion Becomes Imposed Destination
- Reading type: mixed
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (75:10-12) -> P2 (75:29-30) -> P3 (75:33-35); contrast and reversal: attempted escape settles, externally driven movement replaces self-direction, and swaggered travel is overtaken by the destination's superior claim.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 6A and 6C; P2 19A-19B; P3 28B-28C.
- Scene or process: A traveler first seeks a route out, then becomes part of a directed procession, and finally displays control on a household road whose terminal relation remains outside his command.
- Active motifs: flight and place of escape (`quranic:root_001142:B001/m01`); fixed settlement after movement (`quranic:root_001215:B003/m01`); driving or herding bodies forward (`quranic:root_000762:B001/m01`); road, direction, and destination (`quranic:root_001630:B002/m01`); swagger through extended gait (`quranic:root_001432:B006/m01`); superior entitlement and claim (`quranic:root_001684:B008/m01`); terminal limit reached (`quranic:root_000076:B001/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 75:10 `ٱلْمَفَرُّ` (ف ر ر); 75:12 `ٱلْمُسْتَقَرُّ` (ق ر ر); 75:29-30 `ٱلسَّاقُ`, `ٱلْمَسَاقُ` (س و ق); 75:22,24 `وُجُوهٌ` (و ج ه); 75:33 `يَتَمَطَّىٰ` (م ط و); 75:32,34-35 `تَوَلَّىٰ`, `أَوْلَىٰ` (و ل ي); surface anchor unavailable for ء ل ي.
- Synthesis: The repeated route signature changes agency at each boundary: the person chooses flight, the driver imposes a track, and the homeward traveler performs autonomy while a prior claim closes in. This reversal materializes the surah's no-refuge reading by separating freedom of motion from control of arrival.

### S29. P1-P2-P3 Bridge: Questions Change Hands
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (75:6,10) -> P2 (75:27) -> P3 (75:40); role progression and reversal: human questions seek delay and escape, a crisis question seeks an intervener, and the final divine question demands recognition rather than information.
- Canonical scene placements: temporal inquiry at 75:6 (S29); escape cry at P1 6A; healer call at P2 17B; capacity challenge at P3 37B.
- Scene or process: Interrogation moves from when the event will occur, to where flight is possible, to who can heal, before the final question reverses speaker and places divine capacity beyond dispute.
- Active motifs: questioning and requesting (`quranic:root_000661:B001/m01`); spoken utterance (`quranic:root_001272:B001/m01`); sought place of escape (`quranic:root_001142:B001/m01`); incanting healer sought in illness (`quranic:root_000588:B003/m01`); emphatic negation placed under challenge (`quranic:root_001390:B001/m01`); effective capability and dominion (`quranic:root_001205:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 75:6 `يَسْـَٔلُ` (س ء ل); 75:10 `يَقُولُ`, `ٱلْمَفَرُّ` (ق و ل، ف ر ر); 75:27 `قِيلَ`, `رَاقٍ` (ق و ل، ر ق ي); 75:40 `أَلَيْسَ`, `قَٰدِرٍ` (ل ي س، ق د ر).
- Synthesis: The early questions attempt to locate an opening in time, space, or treatment. The last question closes those searches by reversing the interrogative role: it does not expose missing knowledge in the speaker but missing acknowledgment in the hearer.

### S30. P1-P2 Bridge: Complete Assembly from Dispersed Parts
- Reading type: mixed
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (75:3-4) -> P2 (75:17-18); repeated scene signature: dispersed components are collected until none is missing, producing an operational whole rather than a heap.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 2A; P2 13B.
- Scene or process: The same two-stage operation gathers bodily parts into an intact frame and verbal parts into a complete composition that can be read.
- Active motifs: dispersed components joined into one (`quranic:root_000259:B001/m01`); completeness without loss or absent part (`quranic:root_000259:B009/m01`); hard bone as bodily component (`quranic:root_001029:B005/m01`); reading and recitation as textual outcome (`quranic:root_001210:B002/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 75:3 `نَّجْمَعَ`, `عِظَامَهُۥ` (ج م ع، ع ظ م); 75:17 `جَمْعَهُۥ`, `قُرْءَانَهُۥ` (ج م ع، ق ر ء); 75:18 `قَرَأْنَٰهُ`, `قُرْءَانَهُۥ` (ق ر ء).
- Synthesis: The bridge is carried by an exact repeated operation and completeness criterion. Bone and wording remain canonically distinct materials, but each becomes functional only when dispersed units are joined without loss: one bears bodily structure, the other bears recitable order.

### S31. P1-P2-P3 Bridge: Seeing, Exposure, and Refusal to Face
- Reading type: mixed
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (75:7-9) -> P2 (75:22-25) -> P3 (75:32); role progression and reversal: the observer's sight fails, the face becomes both viewing subject and exposed outcome, and the person then tries to break the relation by turning away.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 4A-4B; P2 15A-15C and 16C; P3 28A.
- Scene or process: Visual agency moves from an eye overwhelmed by what it sees, to a countenance that looks and is itself read, to a bodily refusal enacted by redirecting the face and back.
- Active motifs: direct ocular seeing (`quranic:root_000121:B001/m01`); intact eye whose sight has gone (`quranic:root_001273:B021/m01`); face as exposed front (`quranic:root_001630:B001/m01`); sight or insight directed toward an object (`quranic:root_001520:B001/m01`); face turned toward a direction (`quranic:root_001684:B006/m01`); turning the back in refusal (`quranic:root_001684:B007/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 75:7 `ٱلْبَصَرُ` (ب ص ر); 75:1,6 `ٱلْقِيَٰمَةِ` (ق و م); 75:22,24 `وُجُوهٌ` (و ج ه); 75:23 `نَاظِرَةٌ` (ن ظ ر); 75:32 `تَوَلَّىٰ` (و ل ي).
- Synthesis: Seeing does not preserve the observer's control. The eye first loses mastery, the face then carries a publicly legible orientation, and turning away becomes a visible act rather than an escape from visibility. This role reversal binds cosmic sight failure to exposed judgment and embodied refusal.

### S32. P1-P2-P3 Bridge: Appointed Measure Against Haste
- Reading type: mixed
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (75:1,6) -> P2 (75:16-21) -> P3 (75:33,37-40); shared invariant and causal contrast: an event, a received recitation, gestation, and mortality each reach integrity at an assigned measure that premature action cannot advance.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 8C; P2 14A-14B; P3 33C and 37A.
- Scene or process: The severe day has its occurrence, the recipient is restrained from rushing reception, pregnancy extends to completed form, and death arrives as a measured appointment.
- Active motifs: severe event embodied as a day (`quranic:root_001700:B003/m01`); rushing or taking before due time (`quranic:root_000987:B001/m01`); waiting and granting respite (`quranic:root_001520:B002/m01`); appointed time that approaches or is delayed (`quranic:root_001210:B005/m01`); bounded measure, limit, and term (`quranic:root_001205:B001/m01`); gestation extended to completion (`quranic:root_001432:B007/m01`); death as an appointed term (`quranic:root_001450:B003/m01`).
- Ayah anchors: 75:1,6 `يَوْمِ`, `يَوْمُ` (ي و م); 75:16,20 `لِتَعْجَلَ`, `ٱلْعَاجِلَةَ` (ع ج ل); 75:23 `نَاظِرَةٌ` (ن ظ ر); 75:17-18 `قُرْءَانَهُۥ`, `قَرَأْنَٰهُ` (ق ر ء); 75:40 `قَٰدِرٍ` (ق د ر); 75:33 `يَتَمَطَّىٰ` (م ط و); 75:37 `مَّنِىٍّ`, `يُمْنَىٰ` (م ن ي).
- Synthesis: Haste changes the receiver or interrupts development; it does not move the appointed limit. The shared invariant is therefore measured completion, not time in general: event, recitation, body, and death become what they are only by reaching their proper term.
